c < NR; ++c) dst[k * NR + c] = 0.0;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        // implicit barrier: Bpack complete\n\n        AlignedBuf Apack(std::min(MCI, TM) * KC);\n        alignas(64) double edge[MR * NR];\n\n        #pragma omp for collapse(2) schedule(dynamic, 1)\ncmake_minimum_required(VERSION 3.10)\nproject(matmul_benchmark)\n\nset(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD 20)\nset(CMAKE_CXX_STANDARD_REQUIRED ON)\n\nif(NOT CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE)\n  set(CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE Release)\nendif()\n\nfind_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)\n\nadd_executable(matmul matmul.cpp)\n\n# Enable optimizations\n# -ffp-contract=off keeps the kernel's explicit mul/add rounding (FMA only where requested)\ntarget_compile_options(matmul PRIVATE -O3 -march=native -ffp-contract=off -Wall -Wextra)\n\n# Link OpenMP and math library\ntarget_link_libraries(matmul PRIVATE OpenMP::OpenMP_CXX m)","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiGDUseoZddESizTpYBE","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The matmul benchmark now runs in parallel with OpenMP, and its output is bit-for-bit identical to the original program's. I only changed the existing `matmul.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt`, and the executable is still `matmul`.\n\n**Speed on this machine** (2× EPYC 7763, 128 cores / 256 hardware threads):\n\n| N | Original | Now |\n|---|---|---|\n| 2048 | 98.7 s (0.17 GFLOPS) | ~33 ms (~500 GFLOPS) |\n| 4096 | – | ~1.3–1.4 TFLOPS |\n| 8192 | – | ~2.0–2.2 TFLOPS |\n\n**Correctness:** I compared the full `-r` output (sum, min, max, samples and hash) against the original binary for N = 1, 7, 95, 96, 513, 517, 1000, 1025, 1537 and 2048, using 1, 3, 128 and 256 threads. Every case matched exactly, and `-v` validation passes up to N=8192. Results don't depend on the thread count, because each output element still adds up its terms in the original k-order.\n\n**What changed in `matmul.cpp`:**\n- **Multiplication:** the matrix is split into cache-sized blocks and copied into a cache-friendly layout. The B matrix is copied once, in parallel. The inner kernel computes a 6×8 patch of the result with AVX2 instructions.\n- **Work split:** OpenMP hands out rectangles of the result matrix to threads on demand, and both steps share one parallel region. A small cost model picks the rectangle size from N and the thread count. Very small matrices (N < 96) run serially to avoid thread start-up overhead.\n- **Matching the original's rounding:** the original build multiplies and adds k-terms as two separate steps. The only fused multiply-add is the last term when N is odd. The new kernel copies this exactly, which is why the hashes match.\n- **Initialization** also runs in parallel.\n- **Default thread count:** when `OMP_NUM_THREADS` is unset, the program uses one thread per physical core, detected from Linux sysfs. Two threads sharing a core were about 30% slower here because they compete for the same floating-point units. Setting `OMP_NUM_THREADS` overrides this.\n- **Fallback:** a plain C++ kernel with the same rounding is used on CPUs without AVX2.\n\n**`CMakeLists.txt`:** it now requires and links OpenMP (`find_package(OpenMP REQUIRED)`), defaults to a Release build, and adds `-ffp-contract=off`. That flag stops the compiler from fusing the kernel's separate multiply and add back into one instruction.\n\n**Things to know:**\n- **Rounding is tied to this build:** the exact match follows what GCC 13 produced for the original code. A different compiler would have rounded the original differently anyway.\n- **Thread pinning:** pinning threads (`OMP_PROC_BIND=spread OMP_PLACES=cores`) gives a few percent more at large N. Each parallel region also costs several milliseconds when threads aren't pinned. The program can't turn pinning on by itself, so set those variables yourself.\n- **Memory placement:** `A` and `C` sit on the first CPU socket's memory, because `std::vector` zero-fills them from the main thread. That limits scaling across the two sockets a little. I left it alone because the shared results-printing code takes a plain `std::vector`, so a custom allocator isn't an option."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2226,"cache_read_input_tokens":58368,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2226},"output_tokens":5,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"78b01686-9f5c-43c0-b9cb-c3233371b7b1","uuid":"ef1b57d5-0efe-47c5-995d-37bf681ca3d8","timestamp":"2026-10-05T01:59:30.478Z","request_id":"req_011CfiGDUJBTwhiGMJLv11gR"}
